===== PAGE 3 =====
About the Report
The strategic integration of Jet Inks Private Limited significantly expanded market reach, strengthened backward 
integration in consumables, and generated operational synergies that drive revenue growth and margin expansion. As 
manufacturing transitions toward Industry 4.0, Aztec is heavily investing in technology ownership, digital transformation, 
and next-generation printhead technologies to develop indigenous solutions and reduce external supply chain 
dependencies.
Anchored by a healthy order pipeline, growing recurring revenues from consumables and services, and expanding 
opportunities in regulated industries, Aztec continues to strengthen its long-term competitiveness. Driven by India’s 
increasing focus on product authentication, traceability, and manufacturing formalization, the company remains 
committed to helping clients boost productivity, ensure compliance, and enhance operational efficiency
Every manufacturing revolution relies on precision. Founded to redefine 
industrial coding, marking, and traceability, Aztec Fluids & Machinery Limited 
has evolved into one of India’s leading integrated solution providers. Moving 
beyond standard equipment sales, the company combines advanced hardware, 
proprietary consumables, software integration, and comprehensive after-sales 
support to serve thousands of installations across pharmaceuticals, food & 
beverages, FMCG, extrusion, and industrial manufacturing.

===== PAGE 5 =====
Company Overview
Aztec Fluids & Machinery Limited is an integrated 
industrial technology company providing 
coding, marking, serialization, and track & trace 
solutions. Its end-to-end offerings combine 
industrial printers, specialized consumables, 
inks, software, automation, and after-sales 
service to help manufacturers improve product 
identification, regulatory compliance, traceability, 
and operational efficiency. Information on 
its offerings is available via its website and 
corporate disclosures.
Headquartered with manufacturing facilities in 
Gujarat and an expanding nationwide service 
network, Aztec serves diverse sectors including 
pharmaceuticals, food & beverages, FMCG, 
extrusion, packaging, chemicals, agrochemicals, 
and industrial manufacturing. Its solutions 
support critical applications ranging from 
batch coding and expiry printing to full product 
authentication and complete traceability 
systems.
A key strength is its integrated business model: 
combining equipment manufacturing with 
proprietary consumables, software, and technical 
support. This approach drives meaningful 
recurring revenues from consumables and 
services, supporting margin stability and 
long-term customer engagement. Aztec 
continues to enhance its technology through 
R&D, manufacturing infrastructure, and product 
innovation, focusing on backward integration, 
localization, and technology ownership to build 
supply chain resilience and expand indigenous 
capabilities.

===== PAGE 7 =====
Company at a Glance
Deliver innovative coding, marking, and traceability solutions 
that enhance manufacturing efficiency and compliance. 
To be a globally trusted technology partner in coding, marking, 
serialization, and track & trace solutions by delivering innovative, 
reliable, and Make in India solutions that empower industries 
worldwide.
Vision
Mission
Aztec Fluids & Machinery Limited is evolving from a coding and marking solutions provider into a 
technology-led industrial solutions company, with a growing focus on manufacturing, intellectual property 
and digital traceability. With roots in coding consumables, the Company has expanded its capabilities 
across printers, inks, fluids, spares, services and integrated traceability solutions, serving industries such 
as extrusion, food, pharmaceuticals, FMCG, agriculture, industrial automation and packaging. Its growing 
installed base of 8,000+ products across 4,000+ customers provides a foundation for recurring consumables 
and service revenues. The acquisition of Jet Inks has further strengthened its ink manufacturing, R&D 
capabilities, customer reach and backward integration, while investments in manufacturing infrastructure 
and next-generation printhead technology are supporting the Company’s transition towards greater 
technology ownership. Going forward, Aztec aims to deepen localisation, expand its installed base and 
recurring consumables business, and capture opportunities in Track & Trace, serialisation, digital solutions 
and international markets. With this shift from a distribution-led model towards manufacturing, technology 
and IP ownership, the Company is positioning itself to address the rising automation, compliance and 
traceability requirements of modern manufacturing.

Message from the Chairman
“Building the Future. Engineering  
Possibilities. Creating Enduring Value.”
Dear Shareholders,
It gives me immense pleasure to present to you 
the Annual Report of Aztec Fluids & Machinery 
Limited for FY 2025–26.
The year gone by has been an important chapter 
in Aztec’s journey. More than a year of business 
performance, it has been a year of transformation, 
capability building and preparing the organisation 
for the opportunities of tomorrow.
The industrial landscape around us is changing 
rapidly. Manufacturing is becoming smarter and 
more automated. Supply chains are becoming 
increasingly digital. Product traceability and 
authentication are gaining importance, while 
customers are looking for technology partners 
who can offer reliability, integration and complete 
solutions rather than standalone products.
We believe these changes present a significant 
opportunity for Aztec.
Our vision is therefore evolving beyond being a 
manufacturer of coding and marking equipment. 
We are building Aztec into an integrated industrial 
technology company, bringing together hardware, 
consumables, software, data, automation, service 
and engineering capabilities to create meaningful 
solutions for our customers.
From Products to Technology
Technology and innovation will remain at the heart 
of our future.
The integration of Jet Inks has strengthened our 
capabilities in consumables and formulations 
and has created a platform for deeper 
backward integration. Our focus now extends 
towards developing greater control over critical 
technologies, strengthening engineering 
capabilities and progressively increasing 
indigenisation.
We are actively pursuing the development of 
next-generation technologies and strategic 
collaborations in areas such as advanced 
print-head technologies and critical components.
Our objective is clear: to move progressively from 
dependence on external technology ecosystems 
towards creating, owning and continuously 
improving our own intellectual and engineering 
capabilities.
We believe that technological capability will be 
one of the most important sources of competitive 
advantage in the years ahead.
Expanding Our Product Portfolio
One of the most important pillars of our strategy 
is the development of a broader and more 
differentiated product portfolio.
Building upon our existing capabilities across CIJ, 
TIJ, DOD, Laser, TTO and PIJ technologies, we 
intend to expand into new products, technologies 
and applications.
Our future product roadmap includes advanced 
coding and marking systems, laser technologies, 
automation solutions, vision-based inspection, 
traceability and serialization platforms, and 
integrated digital solutions.

===== PAGE 9 =====
We are also strengthening our capabilities in 
areas such as CO2 and fibre laser technologies, 
while exploring opportunities to combine printing, 
inspection, data and automation into integrated 
production-line solutions.
Our ambition is to ensure that Aztec is not simply 
selling a machine, but solving a larger customer 
problem.
The Next Frontier: Digitalisation and Traceability
We see tremendous potential in the convergence 
of coding, traceability, data and digital 
technology.
Manufacturers increasingly need to know where 
a product was manufactured, when it was 
produced, which batch it belongs to and how it 
moves through the supply chain.
This creates an opportunity for Aztec to move 
beyond printing information onto a product 
towards enabling the creation, verification and 
utilisation of product identity and data.
We intend to develop solutions incorporating QR 
codes, serialization, cameras, sensors, software, 
databases and analytics to help customers make 
their production processes more connected, 
transparent and intelligent.
The long-term opportunity lies in combining 
our hardware and consumables expertise 
with software, data and automation to create 
integrated Industry 4.0 solutions.
Building for Scale
Our manufacturing campus is an important part 
of this long-term vision.
The facility has been conceived not merely as 
a manufacturing location, but as a platform for 
future growth, innovation, R&D, automation and 
product development.
As our product portfolio expands, this 
infrastructure will enable us to progressively 
strengthen manufacturing capabilities, improve 
engineering and testing facilities, support product 
development and create greater integration 
between R&D, manufacturing and quality.
We are building capacity not only for the 
business we have today, but for the business we 
aspire to become.
Deeper Customer Relationships
Our installed base provides Aztec with a strong 
foundation for future growth.
However, we see our customer relationships as 
much more than the sale of equipment.
Every machine installed creates an opportunity 
to build a long-term relationship through 
consumables, service, maintenance, upgrades, 
automation and technology solutions.
Our strategy is therefore to move progressively 
from a product-led relationship to a 
lifecycle-based customer relationship.
Our vertical-led approach across sectors such 
as Extrusion, Food, Pharma & FMCG, Industrial 
Automation, Agriculture and other industrial 
segments will allow us to build deeper domain 
expertise and develop solutions tailored to 
specific customer requirements.
We also see significant opportunities in 
institutional, government and automation-led 
projects, as well as in developing our export 
markets.
Our aspiration is to build products in India that 
can compete confidently with established global 
technologies.
Our People: The Foundation of Our Future
No strategy can succeed without the right 
people.
As Aztec grows, developing a future-ready 
workforce and a strong leadership pipeline will be 
one of our highest priorities.
We will continue to invest in technical skills, R&D 
capabilities, leadership development, sales and 
service excellence, digital skills, manufacturing 
expertise, employee engagement and workplace 
safety.
We want Aztec to be an organisation where 
talented people can build careers, develop 
expertise and contribute meaningfully to our 
journey.
We also believe that collaboration across 
functions is essential. The next phase of Aztec 
will require engineers, sales professionals,

It is my privilege to present to you the Group Financial Review for the fiscal 
year ended 31st March 2026. This note aims to offer you a candid, precise and 
comprehensive account of our financial performance, the decisions that shaped 
our capital structure, the quality of our earnings, and the trajectory we have set 
for the years ahead.
Dear Fellow Shareholders,
Message from the CFO
Financial Statements

===== PAGE 12 =====
FY 2025-26 was defined by three converging 
realities: a persistently challenging macroeconomic 
environment characterised by Rupee volatility, 
elevated global inflationary pressures, rising 
procurement costs and cautious capital spending 
by our end-customers; a concerted and deliberate 
investment cycle that we chose to prosecute 
in technology, organisational capability, digital 
infrastructure and strategic acquisitions; and 
the underlying resilience of our business model, 
which continued to demonstrate its ability to 
grow revenue and protect margins even as these 
headwinds prevailed.
The numbers we report today are a function of 
all three. Where headline PAT at the consolidated 
level appears marginally lower year-on-year, I want 
you to understand precisely why and why I regard 
this as a sign of financial maturity rather than any 
deterioration in our business fundamentals.
The Operating Environment: A Year of Deliberate 
Navigation
The external environment in FY 2025-26 presented 
a materially more complex backdrop than the 
preceding year. The Indian Rupee witnessed 
sustained depreciation pressure against the US 
Dollar, driven by persistent global inflationary 
concerns, elevated US interest rates, geopolitical 
instability in West Asia and consequent volatility 
in crude oil prices. For a business that sources a 
meaningful proportion of its critical components 
from 
international 
suppliers, 
this 
directly 
increased our input costs and placed pressure 
on our procurement economics. Simultaneously, 
customer decision-making cycles lengthened. 
Capital expenditure approvals across our client 
base particularly in industrial manufacturing were 
deferred or subject to heightened scrutiny. This 
affected project closures and stretched working 
capital cycles across the industry. Despite these 
conditions, our management team executed with 
precision. Revenue grew. Margins held. Cash was 
generated. And we deployed capital purposefully. 
That we achieved all four simultaneously in this 
environment  is the financial story of FY 2025-26.

===== PAGE 13 =====
The Aztec Group comprising Aztec Fluids & Machinery Limited (parent entity) and Jet Inks Private Limited 
(100% wholly-owned subsidiary) delivered consolidated Revenue from Operations of Rs. 9,653.04 Lakhs 
in FY 2025-26, representing a growth of 9.2% over Rs. 8,842.49 Lakhs in FY 2024-25. This was achieved 
across a diversified revenue base spanning Extrusion (31.4%), Food, Pharma & FMCG (26.2%), Industrial 
Equipment & Automation (12.5%), Agriculture (8.7%) and Packaging (4.6%), with the remainder from a 
portfolio of emerging verticals. 
The marginal decline in PAT and PAT margin is attributable entirely and exclusively to the 85.2% increase 
in depreciation charges (from Rs. 118.67 Lakhs to Rs. 219.81 Lakhs) arising from our deliberate and 
accelerated capital investment programme including the new manufacturing unit at Kanera, technology 
platform deployments and the Salesforce enterprise system. The underlying operating business showed 
no deterioration. EBITDA grew 9.6%, and EBITDA margin expanded by 38 basis points, conclusively 
demonstrating the strength of our core business performance.

===== PAGE 14 =====
The standalone performance of our parent entity Aztec Fluids & Machinery Limited reflects the core strength 
of our coding and marking solutions business. Standalone Revenue from Operations grew a robust 12.9% to 
Rs. 8,337.81 Lakhs in FY 2025-26, from Rs. 7,387.09 Lakhs in FY 2024-25, demonstrating continued market 
acceptance, deeper customer penetration and effective execution of our growth strategy.
Jet Inks Private Limited, our 100% wholly-owned subsidiary acquired during FY 2024-25 using Rs. 1,400 
Lakhs of IPO proceeds, delivered a strong year of profitable growth in FY 2025-26. The acquisition thesis 
backward integration into ink manufacturing, access to an established client base in Southern and Eastern 
India, and the addition of the Bee Jet printer brand has been validated by actual performance. Integration 
was completed within three months with zero operational disruption.

===== PAGE 16 =====
The Group’s balance sheet position as at 31st March 2026 reflects a company at an inflection point one that 
has deployed substantial capital through an ambitious investment cycle, yet emerges with a strengthened 
equity base, manageable leverage and robust liquidity. I will walk you through the key dimensions.
Standalone net worth of Aztec Fluids & Machinery Limited strengthened from Rs. 4,997.76 Lakhs to Rs. 
5,761.97 Lakhs a 15.3% increase driven by retained earnings from profitable operations. At the consolidated 
Group level, net worth stood at Rs. 5,752.80 Lakhs as at 31st March 2026. The growth in reserves and 
surplus is entirely organic, driven by PAT accretion, and reflects the compounding value being created for 
shareholders.
Standalone long-term borrowings have been substantially reduced, with the Company repaying Rs. 35.22 
Lakhs net during the year. Short-term borrowings stood at Rs. 754.46 Lakhs (standalone), used primarily 
for working capital purposes. The Group Debt-to-Equity ratio remains conservative. With the Company’s 
strong operating cash generation profile and no significant debt-funded capital expansion planned in the 
immediate term, I expect leverage to reduce further in FY 2026-27. Notably, Rs. 372 Lakhs of IPO proceeds 
were deployed specifically for debt repayment, as disclosed in the prospectus a commitment we have 
honoured in full.
The FY 2025-26 investment cycle reflected our disciplined approach to growth capital: invest ahead of 
demand, invest in capabilities that create durable competitive advantage, and ensure every rupee deployed 
serves a clear financial rationale.
New Manufacturing Unit: Kanera, Dist. Kheda: The inauguration of our new manufacturing facility represents 
a cornerstone investment in the Company’s capacity to deliver on its backward integration strategy. The 
facility is designed with 80% automation, a production capacity of 50 machines per day and ink output 
capacity of 3,000-4,000 litres per day. Depreciation on this asset has commenced and is reflected in the 
higher depreciation charge in FY26.
Technology Platforms : Investment in enterprise-grade digital infrastructure the first full-scale Salesforce 
deployment in India’s coding and marking industry for real-time pipeline visibility, automated service 
workflows and integrated financial reporting across all 9 regional offices.
Intangible Assets Under Development : Rs. 400.51 Lakhs (standalone): This represents investment in

===== PAGE 23 =====
Technology-Led Organization
Technology lies at the core of Aztec Fluids & Machinery Limited’s growth strategy. Over the years, the 
Company has evolved from supplying industrial coding and marking equipment to developing integrated 
technology solutions that combine hardware, software, automation, consumables, and traceability 
systems. Recognizing the increasing demand for precision, compliance, and operational efficiency across 
manufacturing industries, Aztec continues to invest in product development, indigenous manufacturing, 
software capabilities, and strategic research collaborations. These initiatives support the Company’s 
transition from a distributor of industrial solutions to a technology-driven manufacturing enterprise
Continuous product development remains an important component of Aztec’s technology strategy. The 
Company is focused on strengthening its engineering capabilities and developing solutions that address 
changing application requirements across its customer industries. Strategic collaborations with technology 
partners further complement these efforts by providing access to specialised technologies, technical 
expertise and new product development capabilities. These collaborations enable Aztec to broaden its 
technological portfolio while accelerating the development of solutions suited to emerging requirements in 
industrial coding, marking and traceability.
Indigenous Technology Development
Software-Enabled Industrial Solutions
Research, Product Engineering & Strategic Collaborations
Aztec is progressively strengthening its in-house engineering and manufacturing capabilities, with a focus 
on developing greater ownership over critical technologies and components. The Company’s emphasis 
on indigenous product development and backward integration is particularly relevant to its coding and 
marking business, where product reliability, precision and availability of critical components directly 
influence customer operations. By increasing its manufacturing capabilities and developing technologies 
closer to the core of its business, Aztec aims to improve quality control, reduce dependence on external 
and imported sources, and respond more efficiently to evolving customer requirements. This approach also 
supports the broader objective of building a stronger domestic industrial technology ecosystem in line with 
the Government of India’s Make in India vision.
Modern manufacturing increasingly requires seamless integration between production equipment and 
digital systems. Aztec addresses this requirement by offering software-enabled coding, serialization, and 
track & trace solutions that help manufacturers improve production visibility, ensure product authentication, 
and meet evolving regulatory requirements. These solutions enable customers to manage product 
identification, monitor production processes, and strengthen supply chain traceability through integrated 
digital platforms.

by the transition from standalone coding

===== PAGE 27 =====
LT800 Series — Small Character Inkjet Printer
LT900 Series — Continuous Inkjet Printer
AZ 50121 / AZ 50122 — TIJ Printer Series
The LT800 Series is a small-character continuous inkjet 
printer built around a modular InkCore ink system and a 
new-generation printhead. Running on Linux OS with a 
7-inch touch WYSIWYG interface, it is designed for ease 
of maintenance and consistent print quality across long 
production runs.
The LT900 Series is Aztec’s high-durability continuous 
inkjet platform, purpose-built for demanding industrial 
environments. With self-calibration technology and strong 
EMC compatibility, it maintains superior print quality 
even under continuous multi-shift production, minimizing 
downtime and operator intervention.
The AZ 50121 (single head) and AZ 50122 (double head) 
bring high-definition thermal inkjet printing to production 
lines that demand crisp, small-footprint coding. Built around 
a 7-inch touch panel and an intuitive Windows-based 
interface, the series is designed for operators who need fast 
changeovers and reliable, smudge-free codes on primary 
and secondary packaging.

===== PAGE 28 =====
AZ 8030F / 50F / 60F — CO2 Fiber Laser Printer
AZH12 / AZH25 — Handheld TIJ Printer
LT760 Printer — Small Character Inkjet Printer
Aztec’s CO2 Fiber Laser Printer Series delivers permanent, 
high-precision marking with zero ink, zero makeup fluid, 
and near-zero downtime. The heavy-body construction 
resists vibration for accurate marking even on high-speed 
production lines.
The Handheld TIJ Printer series (AZH12 and AZH25) puts 
industrial-grade coding in the palm of an operator’s hand. 
With a responsive 4.3-inch touch screen and support for 23 
languages, it is ideal for marking cartons, pipes, drums, and 
irregular surfaces that fixed printers cannot easily reach.
The LT760 is built for manufacturers who prioritize stability 
and low cost of ownership. Its stainless steel IP55 housing, 
plug-and-play ink system, and automatic calibration deliver 
dependable, high-utilization coding across food and 
beverage, pharmaceutical, and industrial production lines.

Situated on the Ahmedabad–Vadodara Old Expressway, the facility offers excellent connectivity, providing a 
major logistical advantage for moving raw materials, finished products, and equipment. Its location enables 
efficient access to key industrial and commercial markets across Gujarat and beyond to support expanding 
operations.
Spread across an expansive, continuous 11,000 square metres of cohesive land space unlike fragmented 
industrial facilities the campus provides the flexibility to plan, integrate, and scale infrastructure for future 
expansion and capacity enhancement as the business grows.
The facility creates a unified industrial ecosystem for coding, marking, printing, and associated technologies 
by consolidating manufacturing, assembly, quality assurance, technology, warehousing, and logistics under 
one roof.
In an industry traditionally characterized by smaller, fragmented operating setups, the scale of Aztec’s 
campus represents a distinctive competitive advantage—standing among the most comprehensive setups 
in India’s coding and marking ecosystem.
More than physical space, the campus is an investment in future capabilities:
Operational Excellence: Strengthens manufacturing, improves efficiency, enhances quality control, and 
creates an environment for collaborative product development.
Scalable Foundation: Accommodates future investments in capacity, technology ownership, automation, 
and indigenous innovation.
Long-Term Perspective: Establishes a physical foundation to serve a growing customer base and build a 
world-class Indian enterprise in industrial technology.

===== PAGE 32 =====
Awards, Recognitions and Certifications
At Aztec Fluids & Machinery Limited, our approach to growth is anchored in quality, responsible operations, 
continuous improvement and long-term stakeholder trust. During FY 2025–26, the Company strengthened 
this foundation through a combination of internationally recognised management system certifications, 
ESG assessment and external recognition, reflecting its continued focus on building a responsible and 
future-ready organisation.
A notable milestone during the year was the Company’s Dun & Bradstreet ESG Registered™ Profile, which 
carried an overall ESG Rating of “Medium” (Rating 3). The Company’s Environmental, Social and Governance 
dimensions were each assessed as “Medium”, providing an external assessment of its ESG profile and an 
important reference point for further strengthening its sustainability practices and disclosure framework.
The Company’s commitment to environmental management was reinforced through ISO 14001:2015 
– Environmental Management System certification, covering its defined operations relating to the 
manufacture of printing ink, printers and scanners, including barcode scanners and smart card readers. 
The certification reflects the Company’s adoption of a structured framework for managing environmental 
aspects associated with its operations.
Aztec also holds ISO 45001:2018 – Occupational Health & Safety Management System certification, 
covering its manufacturing activities. The certification reinforces the Company’s focus on maintaining 
structured occupational health and safety practices and providing a safe working environment for its 
employees and other stakeholders.
Quality remains central to Aztec’s manufacturing and customer proposition. Its ISO 9001:2015 – Quality 
Management System certification covers the manufacture and supply of Continuous Inkjet Printers and 
consumables, including Ink, Make-up and Wash solutions. The certification supports the Company’s 
emphasis on process discipline, consistency and quality management across its defined operations.
During the year, Aztec also received a Certificate of Appreciation from Astral Limited for its commitment to 
Environmental Sustainability. The recognition acknowledged the Company’s efforts towards eco-friendly 
practices and responsible e-waste disposal through authorised agencies, providing external appreciation 
of its environmental initiatives.
Further strengthening its corporate credentials, the Company maintained its D-U-N-S® Registered™ 
status with Dun & Bradstreet, providing an internationally recognised business identification that supports 
transparency and credibility in interactions with customers, suppliers, financial institutions and other 
stakeholders.
Together, these certifications and recognitions reflect Aztec’s approach towards building robust processes, 
responsible operations and a culture of continuous improvement. They complement the Company’s 
broader focus on quality, environmental management, workplace safety, ESG practices and stakeholder 
confidence as it continues to scale its business.
Building Trust Through Quality, Responsibility and Continuous Improvement

Aztec’s 
Market Presence
Aztec Fluids & Machinery Limited has built a growing market presence by serving manufacturers across 
a wide range of industries through its portfolio of coding, marking, serialization and traceability solutions. 
Its customer base spans pharmaceuticals, food & beverages, FMCG, packaging, chemicals, automotive, 
electricals, building materials and other industrial sectors where reliable product identification and 
traceability are critical. The Company’s ability to combine equipment, consumables, software and after-sales 
support enables it to address customer requirements across the product lifecycle. 
Within India, Gujarat, Rajasthan and Tamil Nadu are identified as key revenue-contributing markets. Aztec 
is focused on strengthening its presence across other important manufacturing clusters, supported by its 
expanding distribution and service network. This network enables the Company to provide installations, 
technical assistance, preventive maintenance and customer support closer to manufacturing locations, 
while strengthening relationships with customers across diverse industrial segments. 
Aztec is also progressively expanding its international footprint. The Company has established a presence in 
East African markets, while its international strategy is supported by direct exports, distributor partnerships 
and collaborations with global technology partners. The Company’s investor presentation also highlights 
its role as an exclusive distributor for Lead Tech (Zhuhai) Electronic Co. Ltd. in several countries, supporting 
its ability to access international markets. 
The Company’s market expansion is closely linked to the changing requirements of global manufacturing. 
As businesses increasingly adopt automation, traceability and regulatory-compliant identification systems, 
Aztec is positioned to deepen its presence across existing markets while selectively entering new 
geographies. Its growing combination of manufacturing capabilities, technology partnerships, integrated 
consumables, application expertise and service support provides a platform for expanding both domestic 
and international reach.
Expanding Reach Across India and Global Markets

Jet Inks: Strengthening Aztec’s 
Integrated Business Model
Aztec Fluids & Machinery Limited believes 
sustainable growth comes from strengthening 
capabilities across the value chain alongside 
expanding its customer base. The acquisition 
of Jet Inks Private Limited, now a 100% wholly 
owned subsidiary, marks a significant milestone 
in this strategy. It supports Aztec’s evolution 
from a coding and marking equipment supplier 
into an integrated provider of industrial coding, 
marking, serialization, and traceability solutions, 
combining hardware, consumables, software, 
and lifecycle services.
Established in 2002, Jet Inks has built expertise 
in manufacturing compatible inks, additives, 
and consumables for Continuous Inkjet (CIJ) 
printing systems, serving industries including 
food & beverage, pharmaceuticals, packaging, 
pipes, paints, cables, steel, dairy, and other 
manufacturing sectors. Its Bee Jet CIJ and TIJ 
printers, launched in 2022, further strengthened 
its industrial coding and marking portfolio. The 
company has a strong customer base across 
Eastern and Southern India and an expanding 
international presence across Nepal, Bangladesh, 
Africa, and Saudi Arabia.

===== PAGE 40 =====
Core Strategic Positioning
Key Competitive Strengths & Business Drivers
Aztec Fluids & Machinery Limited has evolved from an equipment supplier into an integrated, technology-driven 
solutions provider for industrial coding, marking, serialization, and track & trace. Its model combines 
hardware, proprietary consumables, software, and lifecycle support to enhance client productivity, ensure 
compliance, and boost operational efficiency. This transformation was accelerated by acquiring Jet Inks 
Private Limited, integrating consumables into its core ecosystem to support sustainable, scalable growth.
Integrated Solutions Across the Value Chain
Offers a single-platform portfolio-industrial printers, inks/consumables, Track & Trace solutions, serialization 
software, automation, installation, commissioning, training, preventive maintenance, AMCs, and technical 
support. Serving as a single-point partner simplifies procurement, eliminates integration friction, and 
ensures product compatibility.
Technology & Innovation 
Focuses on R&D, product engineering, software capabilities, and advanced printing technology to meet 
high-speed, high-accuracy, anti-counterfeiting, digital manufacturing, and traceability needs. Combining 
hardware engineering with in-house consumable formulation optimizes print quality and machine reliability.
Backward Integration via Jet Inks Acquisition 
Integrating consumable manufacturing secured greater control over formulation development, quality, 
supply continuity, and costs while reducing external vendor reliance. It expanded distribution networks, 
increased cross-selling, accelerated equipment-ink compatibility, and expanded higher-margin recurring 
revenue streams..
The Aztec Advantage
Diversified Industry Presence 
Serves 15+ sectors-including pharmaceuticals, food & beverage, FMCG, chemicals, packaging, automotive, 
electricals, cables & wires, building materials, and engineering. This diversity insulates the company from 
single-sector downturns and allows customized applications across varied production environments.
Customer-Centric Lifecycle Support 
Manages customer relationships through application assessment, system selection, setup, training, 
maintenance, upgrades, and prompt consumable delivery. A pan-India sales and service network minimizes 
downtime and maximizes client retention and repeat business.
Scalable & High-Margin Revenue Model
 Combines initial capital equipment sales with recurring revenues from consumables, spare parts, software, 
AMCs, and services. Ongoing investments in manufacturing infrastructure, R&D, automation, and digital 
transformation maintain operational excellence while scaling efficiently

===== PAGE 52 =====
The industrial coding and marking industry is undergoing a structural transformation, driven by increasing automation, 
stringent regulatory requirements, growing product traceability needs, and the digitalisation of manufacturing. 
Industries such as pharmaceuticals, food & beverages, FMCG, chemicals, automotive and electronics are increasingly 
adopting advanced coding and marking solutions to improve operational efficiency, ensure compliance, protect 
brands and enhance supply chain visibility. These trends create significant long-term opportunities for Aztec Fluids 
& Machinery Limited, supported by its integrated portfolio of coding equipment, consumables, industrial inks and 
technical services.
Industry Landscape and Market Outlook
The global coding and marking equipment market 
was valued at USD 17.5 billion in 2024 and is 
projected to reach USD 24.9 billion by 2030, 
growing at a CAGR of 6.0%. Growth is supported by 
rising industrial automation, smart manufacturing, 
product traceability, anti-counterfeiting initiatives 
and 
increasing 
adoption 
of 
Industry 
4.0 
technologies.
India’s coding and marking equipment market is 
expected to grow from USD 1.08 billion in 2024 to 
USD 1.70 billion by 2030, registering a CAGR of 7.9%. 
Growth is driven by government initiatives such 
as Make in India and the PLI Scheme, increasing 
factory automation, expanding manufacturing 
capacity and stronger regulatory requirements 
across pharmaceuticals, FMCG, food processing, 
chemicals and automotive industries.

NOTES: 
1. The Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA), vide its 
General Circular No. 20/2020 dated May 05, 2020, 
General Circular No. 02/2021 dated January 13, 
2021, General Circular No. 19/2021 dated December 
08, 2021, General Circular No. 21/2021 dated 
December 14, 2021, General Circular No.2/2022 
dated May 05, 2022, General Circular No. 11/2022 
dated December 28, 2022, General Circular No. 
09/2023 dated September 25, 2023, General 
Circular No. 09/2024 dated 19.09.2024 and General 
Circular No. 03/2025 dated 22.09.2025 (“MCA 
Circulars”) and other applicable circulars issued by 
the Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI) 
including Circular No. SEBI/HO/CFD/CFD-PoD-2/P/
CIR/2023/167 dated October 7, 2023  and Circular 
No. SEBI/HO/CFD/CFD-PoD-2/P/CIR/2024/133 dated 
October 3, 2024 (“SEBI Circulars”), has permitted the 
Companies to conduct the Annual General Meeting 
(‘AGM’) through Video Conferencing (VC) or Other 
Audio Visual Means (OAVM) till September 30, 2026. 
In accordance with the said circulars of MCA, SEBI 
and applicable provisions of the Companies Act, 
2013 (the ‘Act’) and SEBI (Listing Obligations and 
Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 (the 
‘SEBI Listing Regulations’), the 16th AGM of the 
Company shall be conducted through VC/OAVM 
(hereinafter called ‘AGM’). Bigshare Services Private 
Limited will provide facility for voting through remote 
e-voting, participation in the AGM through VC/OAVM 
facility and e-voting during the AGM. The procedure 
for participating in the meeting through VC/OAVM is 
explained at Note below and is also available on the 
website of the Company at www.aztecindia.org. 
2. In terms of sections 101 and 136 of the Act, read 
with the rules made thereunder, the listed companies 
may send the notice of AGM and the annual report, 
including financial statements, boards’ report, etc. by 
electronic mode. Pursuant to the said provisions of 
the Act read with MCA Circulars and SEBI Circular, 
Notice of 16th AGM along with the Annual Report for 
F.Y. 2025-2026 is being sent only through electronic 
mode to those members whose email addresses are 
registered with the Company/depositories. Members 
may note that the Notice of the 16th AGM and Annual 
Report for F.Y. 2025-26 will also be available on the 
Company’s website at www.aztecindia.org, website 
of the stock exchanges i.e., BSE Ltd. (‘BSE’) at www.
bseindia.com and on the website of Bigshare Services 
Private Limited at https://ivote.bigshareonline.com/ . 
In this notice, the term member(s) or shareholder(s) 
are used interchangeably.
3. The deemed venue for 16th AGM shall be the 
Registered Office of the Company at Part H Plinth, 4th 
Floor (Top Floor) Takshashila Square, Nr. Krishnabag 
Four Rd, Maninagar, Ahmedabad, Gujarat - 380028.
4. In terms of Section 152 of the Companies Act, 
2013, Mr. Kumudchandra Vaidhya (DIN: 10382278), 
Director of the Company retires by rotation at the 
ensuing Annual General Meeting and being eligible, 
offers himself for re-appointment. The relevant 
details, pursuant to Regulations 36(3) of the SEBI 
Listing Regulations and Secretarial Standard on 
General Meetings issued by the Institute of Company 
Secretaries of India, in respect of Director seeking re-
appointment/appointment at this AGM is annexed 
as Annexure-1.
5.The explanatory statements as required under 
Section 102(1) of the Companies Act, 2013 (“the 
Act”) relating to the Special Business to be transacted 
at the Annual General Meeting (“AGM”) is annexed 
hereto and forms part of this notice.
6.Pursuant to Regulation 42 of SEBI Listing Regulations, 
the record date will be Friday, 4th day of September, 
2026 for the purpose of Annual General Meeting of 
the Company. 
7.The Company has appointed CS Ravi Kapoor, 
Practicing Company Secretary (Membership No. 
FCS: 2587; CP No: 2407), to act as the Scrutinizer for 
remote e-voting as well as the e-voting on the date 
of the AGM, in a fair and transparent manner.
8.Pursuant to the provisions of the Act, a Member 
entitled to attend and vote at the AGM is entitled 
to appoint a proxy to attend and vote on his/her 
behalf and the proxy need not be a Member of the 
Company. Since this AGM is being held pursuant 
to the MCA Circulars through VC / OAVM, physical 
attendance of Members has been dispensed with. 
Accordingly, the facility for appointment of proxies 
by the Members will not be available for the AGM 
and hence the Proxy Form and Attendance Slip are 
not annexed to this Notice.
9. To support the ‘Green Initiative’, the Company 
requests those Members who have not yet registered 
their e-mail address, to register the same directly 
with their DP, in case shares are held in electronic 
form and to the Company, in case shares are held 
in physical form. Further, members holding shares in 
electronic form are requested to notify the changes 
in the above particulars, if any, directly to their 
Depository Participants (DP).
10.Institutional / Corporate Shareholders (i.e. other 
than individuals / HUF, NRI, etc.) are required to 
send a scanned copy (PDF/JPG Format) of its Board 
or governing body Resolution/Authorization etc., 
authorizing its representative to attend the AGM 
through VC / OAVM on its behalf and to vote through

THE INSTRUCTIONS OF SHAREHOLDERS FOR 
E-VOTING AND JOINING VIRTUAL MEETINGS ARE 
AS UNDER:
i. The remote e-voting period begins on Monday, 
7th September, 2026 at 09:00 a.m. and ends on 
Wednesday, 9th September, 2026 at 5:00 p.m. 
During this period shareholders of the Company, 
holding shares either in physical form or in 
dematerialized form, as on the cut-off date (record 
date) of Thursday, 3rd September, 2026 may cast 
their vote electronically. The remote e-voting module 
shall be disabled by Bigshare for voting thereafter.
ii. Shareholders who have already voted prior to the 
meeting date would not be entitled to vote at the 
meeting venue.
iii.Pursuant to SEBI Circular No. SEBI/HO/CFD/CMD/
CIR/P/2020/242 dated 09.12.2020, under Regulation 
44 of Securities and Exchange Board of India 
(Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) 
Regulations, 2015, listed entities are required to 
provide remote e-voting facility to its shareholders, 
in respect of all shareholders’ resolutions. However, it 
has been observed that the participation by the public 
non-institutional shareholders/retail shareholders is 
at a negligible level.
Currently, there are multiple e-voting service 
providers (ESPs) providing e-voting facility to listed 
entities in India. This necessitates registration on 
various ESPs and maintenance of multiple user IDs 
and passwords by the shareholders.
In order to increase the efficiency of the voting 
process, pursuant to a public consultation, it has been 
decided to enable e-voting to all the demat account 
holders, by way of a single login credential, through 
their demat accounts/ websites of Depositories/ 
Depository Participants. Demat account holders

with all e-Voting Service Providers. Click on BIGSHARE and you will be re-directed to i-vote (E-voting

during the meeting.

Policy on Materiality of Related Party Transactions 
and also on dealing with Related Party Transactions 
of the Company (“the Policy”), the material related 
party transactions to be entered by the Company on 
arm’s length basis Promoters requires prior approval 
of the members of the Company through an ordinary 
resolution. In accordance with Regulation 23 of the 
SEBI Listing Regulations, “Material Related Party 
Transaction” means any transaction with a related 
party if the transaction/transactions to be entered 
into individually or taken together with previous 
transactions during a financial year, exceeds ten 
percent of the annual consolidated turnover as per 
the last audited financial statements of the Company 
(“material related party transaction limit”).

2. State of Affairs of the Company:
Aztec Fluids & Machinery Limited, a leading 
manufacturers, exporter and suppliers of a quality 
assured assortment of Printers, Printer consumables 
and Printer Spares. Our product range includes 
Continuous Inkjet (CIJ), Thermal Transfer Overprint 
(TTO), Drop on Demand (DOD) and Laser printers. 
These products are highly acclaimed by our clients, 
based in the markets of the Indian Subcontinent, 
East Asia, Middle East and South East Asia, for 
their distinguished features like supreme quality, 
excellent performance, long functional life and easy 
operations, is detailed in the Management Discussion 
and Analysis Report, which forms part of the Annual 
Report.
Operational Overview:
During the financial year 2025–26, Aztec focused on 
strengthening its operational performance through 
sustainability, efficiency, and supply chain resilience. 
The Company continued to enhance its operational 
efficiencies by advancing its green initiatives, including 
increased adoption of renewable energy, integration 
of green chemistry principles and optimized resource 
management. 

===== PAGE 86 =====
Financial Statements
84
AZTEC
Corporate Overview
Statutory Reports
A strategic focus was maintained on high-margin, 
value-accretive business segments. This included 
scaling fee-for-service (FFS) contracts and expanding 
the manufacturing of niche specialty ingredients, 
thereby improving profitability and long-term financial 
performance. Through these integrated efforts, Aztec 
strengthened its operational backbone, improved 
supply chain reliability, and has positioned itself for 
sustainable, value-driven growth for the years ahead.
3.PERFORMANCE HIGHLIGHTS :
The 
Standalone 
and 
Consolidated 
Financial 
Statements of the Company have been prepared in 
accordance with the Standards on Auditing (“SAs”) 
specified under Section 143 (10) of the Companies 
Act, 2013 (the “Act”), as amended and the Securities 
and Exchange Board of India (Listing Obligations 
and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 
(“SEBI Listing Regulations”).
STANDALONE FINANCIAL RESULTS:
The continuous efforts of management led to fruitful 
and positive results. During the year under review, 
Revenue from Operations has gone up to Rs. 8337.81 
Lakhs against Rs.7387.09 Lakhs in the previous year. 
As a result, the profit before interest, depreciation, 
managerial remuneration, taxation has reached a level 
of the figures which has gone up to Rs. 1484.87 Lakhs 
against Rs. 1384.48 Lakhs for the previous year. The 
profit for the year has also gone up to Rs. 764.21 Lakhs 
against Rs. 736.49 Lakhs for the previous year. After 
adjusting all amounts balance of Rs 4401.97 Lakhs 
has been carried forward to the next year.
Your Directors are making all the possible efforts 
to expand the business of the company, optimize 
expenses and hereby hope to make better performance 
in the years to come.
CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL PERFORMANCE:
On a consolidated basis, the total income for the year 
was INR 9653.04 Lakhs, compared to INR 8,842.49 
Lakhs in FY 2024–25, registering a growth of 8.4%. This 
includes consolidated Revenue from operations of INR 
9653.04 Lakhs and other income of INR 86.70 Lakhs. 
As a result, the profit before interest, depreciation, 
managerial remuneration, taxation has reached a level 
of the figures which has gone up to Rs. 1594.90 Lakhs 
against Rs. 1521.39 Lakhs for the previous year. The 
profit for the year has decrease up to Rs. 740.71 Lakhs 
against Rs. 745.60 Lakhs for the previous year. After 
adjusting all amounts balance of Rs. 4392.80 Lakhs 
has been carried forward to the next year.

Pursuant to the provisions of Section 134(5) of the 
Companies Act, 2013, the board of directors, to the 
best of their knowledge and ability confirm and state 
that – 
i. In the preparation of the annual accounts, the 
applicable accounting standards had been followed 
along with proper explanation relating to material 
departures;
ii. The Directors had selected such accounting policies 
and applied them consistently and made judgments 
and estimates that are reasonable and prudent so as 
to give a true and fair view of the state of affairs of 
the Company at the end of the financial year and of 
the loss of the company for that period;
iii. The Directors have taken proper and sufficient 
care for the maintenance of adequate accounting 
records in accordance with the provisions of this 
Act for safeguarding the assets of the Company 
and for preventing and detecting fraud and other 
irregularities;
iv.The Directors had prepared the annual accounts on 
a ‘going concern’ basis;
v. The Directors had laid down internal financial 
controls to be followed by the Company and that 
such internal financial controls are adequate and 
were operating effectively; and 
vi. The Directors had devised proper systems to ensure 
compliance with the provisions of all applicable laws 
and that such systems were adequate and operating 
effectively.

31.Risk management
In today’s economic environment, risk management 
is a very important part of the business. The main aim 
of risk management is to identify, monitor and take 
precautionary measures in respect of the events that 
may pose risks for the business. Your Company’s risk 
management is embedded in the business processes. 
Your Company has identified certain risks like price 
risk, uncertain global economic environment, interest 
rate, human resource, competition, compliance and 
industrial health and safety risk and also planned 
to manage such risk by adopting best management 
practice.

In terms of Section 177(9) and Section 177(10) of the 
Act the Board of Directors of the Company adopted 
a Whistle Blower Policy/Vigil Mechanism inter alia to 
provide a mechanism for Directors and Employees 
of the Company to approach the Internal Complaint 
Committee and to report instances of unethical 
behaviour, actual or suspected, fraud or violation of 
the Company’s Code of Conduct and other genuine 
concerns related to the Company and provide 
for adequate safeguards against victimization 
of Director(s) or employee(s) who report genuine 
concerns under the mechanism.
The Whistle Blower Policy/ Vigil Mechanism Policy of 
the Company is available on the Company’s Website 
at www.aztecindia.org

41.CODE OF CONDUCT
The Board of Directors has adopted a code of 
conduct which is applicable to the members of the 
board and all employees in the course of day to day 
business operations of the Company. The code has 
been posted on the Company’s website at www.
aztecindia.org.
The code lays down the standard procedure of 
business conduct which is expected to be followed 
by the Directors and the designated employees in 
their business and in particular on matters relating 
to integrity in the work place, in business practices 
and in dealing with stakeholders. 
The Code gives guidance through examples on the 
expected behavior from an employee in a given 
situation and the reporting structure. All the Board 
Members and the Senior Management Personnel 
have confirmed compliance with the Code. All 
Management Staff were given appropriate training 
in this regard.
42.TRANSFER 
TO 
INVESTOR 
EDUCATION 
& 
PROTECTION FUND 
In accordance with the applicable provisions of 
Companies Act, 2013 (hereinafter referred to as “the 
act”) read with Investor Education and Protection 
Fund (accounting, audit, transfer and refund) Rules, 
2016 (hereinafter referred to as the “IEPF rules”), all 
unclaimed dividends are required to be transferred 
by the Company to the IEPF, after completion of 
seven (7) years. Further, according to IEPF rules, the 
shares on which dividend has not been claimed by 
the shareholders for seven (7) consecutive years or 
more shall be transferred to the demat account of the 
IEPF authority, but there is no such unpaid dividend 
of last seven years, so this clause is not applicable to 
the Company.

45.PREVENTION OF INSIDER TRADING
The Company has adopted a code of conduct 
for prohibition of Insider Trading (the ‘code’) in 
accordance with the requirements of the SEBI 
(Prohibition of Insider Trading) Regulations, 2015, 
with a view to regulate trading in securities by the 
Board of Directors and employees of the company, 
their immediate relatives and other insiders as 
defined in the code. During the closure of the trading 
window, no employee/ designated person is permitted 
to trade in the securities of the Company with or 
without pre-clearance as informed by the secretarial 
department, from time to time. Timely disclosures 
are made to the stock exchanges by the Company. 
No employee/ designated person is permitted 
to communicate, provide, or allow access to any 
unpublished price sensitive information relating to 
Company, its securities or any other company (listed 
or proposed to be listed), to any person except where 
such communication is in furtherance of legitimate 
purpose, performance of duties or discharge of legal 
obligations. The Company periodically monitors and 
ensures compliance with the SEBI (Prohibition of 
Insider Trading) Regulations, 2015.

statutory Requirements.

SECRETARIAL AUDIT REPORT
FOR THE FINANCIAL YEAR ENDED 31ST MARCH, 2026
[Pursuant to section 204(1) of the Companies Act, 2013 and rule no. 9 of the Companies (Appointment and 
Remuneration of Managerial Personnel) Rules, 2014]
To,
The Members
AZTEC FLUIDS & MACHINERY LIMITED 
We have conducted the secretarial audit of the 
compliance of applicable statutory provisions and the 
adherence to good corporate practices by Aztec Fluids & 
Machinery Limited (hereinafter called the “Company”). 
Secretarial Audit was conducted in a manner that 
provided us a reasonable basis for evaluating the 
corporate 
conducts/statutory 
compliances 
and 
expressing my opinion thereon.
Based on our verification of the books, papers, minute 
books, forms and returns filed and other records 
maintained by the Company and also the information 
provided by the Company, its officers, agents and 
authorized representatives during the conduct of 
secretarial audit, we hereby report that in our opinion, 
the Company, during the audit period covering the 
financial year ended on 31st March, 2026 complied 
with the statutory provisions listed hereunder and also 
that the Company has proper Board-processes and 
compliance-mechanism in place to the extent, in the 
manner and subject to the reporting made hereinafter:
We have examined the books, papers, minute books, 
forms and returns filed and other records maintained 
by the Company for the financial year ended on 31st 
March, 2026 and verified the provisions of the following 
acts and regulations and also their applicability as far 
as the Company is concerned during the period under 
audit:
i) The Companies Act, 2013 (the Act) and the rules 
made thereunder;
ii) The Securities Contracts (Regulation) Act, 1956 
(“SCRA”) and the rules made thereunder; 
iii)The Depositories Act, 1996 and the Regulations and 
Bye-laws framed thereunder;
iv)The Foreign Exchange Management Act, 1999 and 
the rules and regulations made thereunder to the 
extent of Foreign Direct Investment, Overseas Direct 
Investment and External Commercial Borrowings to 
the extent of their applicability to the Company;
v) The following Regulations and Guidelines prescribed 
under the Securities and Exchange Board of India 
Act, 1992 (‘SEBI Act’): -
(a) The Securities and Exchange Board of India 
(Substantial Acquisition of Shares and Takeovers) 
Regulations, 2011;
(b) The Securities and Exchange Board of India 
(Prohibition of Insider Trading) Regulation, 2015;
(c) The Securities and Exchange Board of India 
(Issue of Capital and Disclosure Requirements) 
Regulations, 2018; 
(d) Securities And Exchange Board of India (Share 
Based Employee Benefits and Sweat Equity) 
Regulations, 2021; 
(e) The Securities and Exchange Board of India (Issue 
and Listing of Non-Convertible Debt Securities) 
Regulations, 2021; 
(f) The Securities and Exchange Board of India 
(Registrar to an Issue and Share Transfer Agents) 
Regulations, 1993 regarding the Companies Act 
and dealing   with clients; 
(g) The Securities and Exchange Board of India 
(Delisting of Equity Shares) Regulations, 2021; 
and
(h) The Securities and Exchange Board of India 
(Buyback of Securities) Regulations, 2018; 
We have also examined compliance with applicable 
clauses of the following
1. Secretarial Standards issued by the Institute of 
Company Secretaries of India.
2. Provisions of SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure 
Requirements) Regulations, 2015.
During the period under review the Company has 
complied with the provisions of the Act, Rules, 
Regulations, Guidelines, Standards, etc. mentioned 
above.
We further report that:
i) The Board of Directors of the Company is duly 
constituted with proper balance of Executive Director, 
Non-Executive Directors and Independent Directors.
ii) Adequate notice is given to all directors to schedule 
the Board Meetings, agenda and detailed notes on 
agenda were sent at least seven days in advance, 
and a system exists for seeking and obtaining further 
information and clarifications on the agenda items 
before the meeting and for meaningful participation 
at the meeting.
iii)All decisions at Board Meetings & Committee 
Meetings are carried out unanimously as recorded in 
the minutes of the meetings of the Board of Directors 
or the Committees of the Board, as the case may be.

MANAGEMENT DISCUSSION AND ANALYSIS REPORT
Aztec Fluids & Machinery Limited ·  Annual Report FY2025-26  ·  BSE SME: 544177
Company Overview and Business Description
Aztec Fluids and Machinery Limited is India’s foremost 
integrated coding, marking, and industrial traceability 
company, serving manufacturers across technology 
platforms that encode, connect, and authenticate 
product identity at every stage of the supply chain. 
Founded over two decades ago and headquartered in 
Ahmedabad, Gujarat, Aztec has built an unparalleled 
market presence across the Extrusion, Pharmaceutical, 
Food and Beverages, FMCG, Chemical, and Automotive 
sectors, with over 1,500 active customer relationships 
spanning the length and breadth of India. The 
Company’s wholly owned subsidiary, Jet Inks Pvt Ltd, 
Chennai, is engaged in the manufacture and supply 
of industrial inks, ribbons, and coding consumables, 
providing Aztec with a unique degree of vertical 
integration that reinforces supply chain resilience, 
ink quality consistency, and margin depth that few 
competitors in the domestic market can match. In 
FY2025-26, Jet Inks contributed meaningfully to 
consolidated group revenue to the tune of Rs. 19.30 
Crores and has been fully restored to operational 
capacity.
Aztec’s strategic positioning is guided by one 
overarching ambition: “From Products to Solutions.” 
This transformation from a hardware supplier to a 
connected solutions platform defines every commercial, 
technological, and capital allocation decision the 
Company makes, and frames the multiyear growth 
thesis that the Board presents to shareholders and the 
investment community. The Company is also positioned 
to benefit from the rapid acceleration in Industry 4.0 
adoption among mid sized and large manufacturers 
seeking real time visibility into production line 
performance, alongside increasing demand across 
pharmaceuticals, food, and chemicals.
Structural Demand Drivers
Regulatory mandates creating noncyclical demand
India’s pharmaceutical serialisation programme, food 
traceability framework, and marking requirements 
for chemicals, electrical goods, and construction 
materials create compliance driven demand pools that 
are structurally immune to economic cycles. These 
are mandates, not preferences, and they collectively 
represent the largest single driver of addressable 
demand growth for Aztec’s core business pillars over 
the coming decade.
PLI driven capacity expansion
India’s Production Linked Incentive schemes across 
pharmaceuticals, food processing, and automotive 
sectors have catalysed significant greenfield and 
brownfield manufacturing investment. Every new 
production line established by a PLI beneficiary is a 
potential new entrant into Aztec’s addressable market 
and an opportunity to begin a relationship that, over 
time, can span the complete solution portfolio.
Counterfeiting and supply chain integrity pressure
The economic and reputational cost of product 
counterfeiting and supply chain adulteration is 
accelerating corporate investment in authentication 
and traceability infrastructure across pharmaceuticals, 
FMCG, and premium consumer products.
Export compliance requirements
Indian 
manufacturers 
exporting 
to 
regulated 
international markets face destination country product 
identification and traceability mandates as a condition 
of market access. Aztec’s solutions in this pillar are 
increasingly positioned not as a cost of compliance, but 
as an enabler of export market entry, transforming a 
regulatory obligation into a competitive capability.
The Long Term Value Creation Thesis
The 
strategic 
value 
of 
this 
transformational 
architecture lies in how its components compound. At 
each successive step, a customer adopts and deepens 
its engagement with Aztec, broadening the recurring 
revenue base and raising the cost and complexity of 
switching to any alternative. This is the architecture of 
long term, durable value creation, where the customer 
relationship strengthens over time rather than being 
renegotiated at every procurement cycle. The Board 
believes that this model of embedded, recurring, 
compliance linked revenue represents a fundamentally 
superior business compared with the transactional 
hardware model that defined the Company’s first two 
decades.
Industry Environment and Strategic Context
India’s industrial coding and marking sector is at 
an inflection point, driven by converging forces: 
rising domestic manufacturing output under the 
Government’s Make in India and Production Linked 
Incentive initiatives, alongside an increasingly complex 
domestic regulatory framework mandating product 
traceability across goods. This is a market that is 
growing in urgency with every high profile product 
recall or regulatory enforcement action, and Aztec’s 
solutions for product traceability and authentication are 
positioned directly at the intersection of this demand.
Competitive Positioning
The Indian coding and marking market is served 
by a combination of global MNC subsidiaries and a 
fragmented domestic tier. Aztec occupies a distinctive 
strategic position between these two segments, 
offering the technological breadth and national service 

===== PAGE 109 =====

Statutory Reports
network of a platform company while retaining the 
pricing agility, customer intimacy, and regulatory 
knowledge that global players structurally cannot 
provide. 
The 
Company’s 
accumulated 
customer 
relationships, proprietary consumables manufacturing 
through Jet Inks, and the depth of compliance expertise 
embedded in its teams represent a competitive moat 
that cannot be replicated by capital expenditure alone. 
As Aztec introduces future business pillars into this 
installed base, the moat deepens further because the 
value the Company delivers becomes embedded in the 
customer’s operational and regulatory infrastructure, 
not merely in their equipment portfolio.
Financial Performance Review FY2025-26
The financial year ended 31 March 2026 was a year of 
operational consolidation and strategic repositioning 
for Aztec. The Company delivered steady consolidated 
revenue growth, maintained a conservative balance 
sheet, and made significant investments in product 
development 
and 
technology 
infrastructure 
in 
preparation for the platform led growth phase that lies 
ahead.
Consolidated Income Statement Highlights
Revenue Analysis
Consolidated revenue of INR 96.53 Cr reflects broad 
based demand across Aztec’s served verticals, with 
major consumption driven sectors contributing the 
largest share of incremental growth. Standalone 
performance represents sustained momentum across 
the core hardware, ink, and service business, while 
Jet Inks Pvt Ltd returned to full operational capacity 
during the year, contributing to consolidated group 
performance.

Margin Performance
The EBITDA margin of 14.33% in FY2025-26 represents 
the Company’s current profitability profile, one that the 
Board expects to expand materially over the medium 
term as the solutions portfolio gains commercial 
traction. The structural driver of this expansion is 
portfolio mix. The solutions portfolio that Aztec intends 
to introduce would carry fundamentally higher gross 
margins than the hardware business that currently 
dominates the revenue mix. As the weight of these 
solutions increases within the consolidated revenue 
base, blended margins will improve not through cost 
reduction, but through the natural economics of a 
higher value, recurring revenue portfolio.
Balance Sheet Strength
The balance sheet remains conservatively structured, 
with a debt to equity ratio of 0.29. Interest coverage is 
robust and finance costs are well managed, confirming 
that the Company’s borrowings present no near term 
refinancing risk. Net Worth of INR 57.53 Cr provides a 
solid equity foundation for the investment and growth 
phase the Board has envisaged in the near future.

Risk Factors and Mitigation Framework
The Board and senior management actively monitor 
the principal risks to strategy execution and operational 
performance. The following reflects the key risk areas 
considered most relevant to Aztec’s current phase of 
development, along with the management response 
and mitigation framework in place for each.
Competitive Intensification Risk
The coding and marking sector in India is subject 
to ongoing competitive dynamics from global MNC 
subsidiaries with significant marketing and R&D 
budgets, from a fragmented domestic tier that

Statutory Reports
competes primarily on price, and from the potential 
emergence of well capitalised new entrants as the 
market’s growth profile attracts greater attention. As 
Aztec moves up the value chain toward managed 
services and connected intelligence, it will also 
encounter a different competitive set, including 
enterprise software and industrial IoT companies that 
do not have Aztec’s hardware and service heritage, but 
that may possess sophisticated technology platforms 
and existing enterprise relationships. The risk of 
competitive intensification is real, and the Board takes 
it seriously. The strategic response, however, is not to 
compete on price or to replicate what competitors offer. 
It is to build a category of business that competitors 
structurally cannot replicate.
Installed base as a structural moat
Aztec’s 1,500+ customer relationships, built over more 
than two decades of plant floor presence, represent 
a competitive asset that cannot be acquired through 
capital deployment. No competitor, global MNC 
or domestic challenger, can replicate the depth of 
operational understanding, compliance knowledge, 
and service trust that Aztec has accumulated.
This installed base is the foundation on which the 
future business model shall evolve.
Vertical integration
Aztec’s ownership of Jet Inks Pvt Ltd creates a 
proprietary 
consumables 
capability 
across 
inks, 
ribbons, and coding materials that generates a 
natural commercial lock in within the installed base. A 
customer who uses Aztec hardware and Aztec inks is 
structurally less exposed to competitive displacement 
than one who sources consumables from third parties. 
This integration is a competitive advantage that is 
difficult to replicate and that compounds in value as the 
installed base grows.
Deepening value over time
The most enduring competitive protection Aztec has 
is not the complexity of changing providers. It is the 
genuine value that the platform delivers as it becomes 
more deeply woven into a customer’s operations. 
When a customer’s production fleet is connected 
to Aztec’s monitoring environment, when their 
compliance workflows are supported through Aztec’s 
managed services, and when their brand protection 
needs are addressed through Aztec’s authentication 
solutions, the relationship naturally deepens because 
the outcomes delivered become more meaningful with 
each passing year. Aztec’s ambition is not to make it 
difficult for customers to leave; it is to make it genuinely 
valuable for them to stay. The Company is building a 
business where the more a customer engages with 
the platform, the more relevant and indispensable 
it becomes, not through restriction, but through the 
compounding value of a relationship built on trust, 
capability, and consistent outcomes.
Regulatory expertise as a differentiator
The compliance landscape that Aztec’s Track and 
Trace solution addresses is complex, evolving, and 
deeply specific to the Indian regulatory environment. 
The knowledge embedded in Aztec’s teams of CDSCO 
requirements, FSSAI frameworks, BIS mandates, and 
the practical implementation realities of each is not 
a capability that technology companies or global 
MNCs can quickly or easily develop. This regulatory 
expertise is a form of competitive moat that becomes 
more valuable, not less, as the regulatory framework 
becomes more complex.
Outlook and Forward Looking Statement
The Board and Management enter FY2026-27 with 
grounded confidence in the strategic direction Aztec 
has chosen and in the operational foundations being 
put in place to execute it. The demand environment 
for Aztec’s products and solutions is structurally 
supportive, underpinned by regulatory mandates that 
are independent of economic cycles, manufacturing 
investment that is being accelerated by Government 
policy, and the growing recognition among Indian 
manufacturers that product traceability and supply 
chain integrity are not compliance costs but commercial 
necessities. What gives the Board confidence in this 
programme is not the scale of the ambition, but the 
quality of the foundation. Aztec’s installed base is 
real. Its customer relationships are deep. Its regulatory 
knowledge is proprietary. Its vertical integration 
through Jet Inks is a structural advantage. Its balance 
sheet is conservatively managed. These are not 
aspirational assets. They are the existing commercial 
reality on which the future business will be built.

Opinion
We have audited the standalone financial statements 
of AZTEC FLUIDS & MACHINERY LIMITED (Formerly 
known as ‘AZTEC FLUIDS & MACHINERY PRIVATE 
LIMITED’) (“the Company”), which comprise the 
standalone balance sheet as at 31st March 2026, 
and the standalone statement of profit and loss and 
standalone statement of cash flows for the year 
then ended, and notes to the standalone financial 
statements, including a summary of significant 
accounting policies and other explanatory information.
In our opinion and to the best of our information and 
according to the explanations given to us, the aforesaid 
standalone financial statements give the information 
required by the Companies Act, 2013 (“Act”) in the 
manner so required and give a true and fair view in 
conformity with the accounting principles generally 
accepted in India, of the state of affairs of the Company 
as at 31st March 2026, its profit, and its cash flows for 
the year ended on that date.
Basis for Opinion
We conducted our audit in accordance with the 
Standards on Auditing (SAs) specified under section 
143(10) of the Act. Our responsibilities under those 
Standards are further described in the Auditor’s 
Responsibilities for the Audit of the Standalone 
Financial Statements section of our report. We are 
independent of the Company in accordance with the 
Code of Ethics issued by the Institute of Chartered 
Accountants of India together with the ethical 
requirements that are relevant to our audit of the 
standalone financial statements under the provisions of 
the Act and the Rules thereunder, and we have fulfilled 
our other ethical responsibilities in accordance with 
these requirements and the Code of Ethics. We believe 
that the audit evidence we have obtained is sufficient 
and appropriate to provide a basis for our opinion on 
the standalone financial statements.
Key Audit Matters
Key audit matters are those matters that, in our 
professional judgment, were of most significance in 
our audit of the standalone financial statements of 
the current period. These matters were addressed in 
the context of our audit of the standalone financial 
statements as a whole, and in forming our opinion 
thereon, and we do not provide a separate opinion on 
these matters. 
We have nothing to communicate in this regard. 
Other Information
The Company’s Board of Directors is responsible for 
the preparation of the other information. The other 
information comprises the information included in 
the annual report, but does not include the Financial 
Statements and our auditor’s report thereon. The 
annual report is expected to be made available to us 
after the date of this auditor’s report.
Our opinion on the standalone financial statements 
does not cover the other information and we will not 
express any form of assurance conclusion thereon.
In connection with our audit of the standalone financial 
statements, our responsibility is to read the other 
information identified above when it becomes available 
and, in doing so, consider whether the other information 
is materially inconsistent with the standalone financial 
statements or our knowledge obtained in the audit, or 
otherwise appears to be materially misstated.
When we read the annual report, if we conclude 
that there is a material misstatement therein, we are 
required to communicate the matter to those charged 
with governance and take necessary actions, as 
applicable under the relevant laws and regulations.

with Governance for the Standalone Financial

The Company’s Board of Directors is responsible 
for the matters stated in section 134(5) of the Act 
with respect to the preparation of these standalone 
financial statements that give a true and fair view of

Auditor’s Responsibilities for the Audit of the 
Standalone Financial Statements
Our objectives are to obtain reasonable assurance 
about whether the standalone financial statements 
as a whole are free from material misstatement, 
whether due to fraud or error, and to issue an 
auditor’s report that includes our opinion. Reasonable 
assurance is a high level of assurance, but is not a 
guarantee that an audit conducted in accordance 
with SAs will always detect a material misstatement 
when it exists. Misstatements can arise from fraud 
or error and are considered material if, individually or 
in the aggregate, they could reasonably be expected 
to influence the economic decisions of users taken on 
the basis of these standalone financial statements.
 
As part of an audit in accordance with SAs, we 
exercise 
professional 
judgment 
and 
maintain 
professional skepticism throughout the audit. We 
also:
•      Identify and assess the risks of material misstatement 
of the standalone financial statements, whether 
due to fraud or error, design and perform audit 
procedures responsive to those risks, and obtain 
audit evidence that is sufficient and appropriate 
to provide a basis for our opinion. The risk of not 
detecting a material misstatement resulting from 
fraud is higher than for one resulting from error, 
as fraud may involve collusion, forgery, intentional 
omissions, misrepresentations, or the override of 
internal control.
•   Obtain an understanding of internal control relevant 
to the audit in order to design audit procedures that 
are appropriate in the circumstances. Under section 
143(3)(i) of the Companies Act, 2013, we are also 
responsible for expressing our opinion on whether 
the company has adequate internal financial 
controls with reference to financial statements 
in place and the operating effectiveness of such 
controls.
•   Evaluate the appropriateness of accounting policies 
used and the reasonableness of accounting 
estimates and related disclosures made by 
management and Board of Directors.
•  Conclude on the appropriateness of management 
and Board of Directors use of the going concern 
basis of accounting in preparation of standalone 
financial statements and, based on the audit 
evidence obtained, whether a material uncertainty 
exists related to events or conditions that may 
cast significant doubt on the Company’s ability to 
continue as a going concern. If we conclude that 
a material uncertainty exists, we are required to 
draw attention in our auditor’s report to the related 
disclosures in the standalone financial statements 
or, if such disclosures are inadequate, to modify our 
opinion. Our conclusions are based on the audit 
evidence obtained up to the date of our auditor’s 
report. However, future events or conditions may 
cause the Company to cease to continue as a going 
concern.
• Evaluate the overall presentation, structure and 
content of the standalone financial statements, 
including

and

underlying transactions and events in a manner 
that achieves fair presentation.
